<meta>This page continues a discussion on the sympathetic relationship between the human spirit and the celestial bodies, specifically how certain activities and states of mind align us with particular planets.</meta>

But everywhere remember that through the affection and study of the mind, and through the very quality of the **spirit** <note>original: *spiritus*. In Renaissance medicine, this refers to a subtle "vapor" in the blood that links the physical body to the immaterial soul</note>, we are very easily and suddenly exposed to the planets that signify that same affection, study, and quality.

Therefore, we are subjected to **Saturn** through separation from human affairs, through leisure, solitude, and constancy; through theology and more secret philosophy, through devotion, magic, and agriculture; and through sorrow. 

We are subjected to **Jupiter** through civic and ambitious business, through natural and common philosophy, through public religion, and through laws. 

To **Mars** through anger and struggles. 

To the **Sun** and **Mercury** through the study of eloquence, and through song, truth, glory, and cleverness. 

To **Venus** through joy, music, and festivities. 

To the **Moon** through a diet similar to plants <note>The author likely refers to a diet of moist, leafy, or simple foods that reflect the Moon's perceived "moist" and "growing" nature</note>. 

But keep this distinction in mind: the more public and grand exercise of talent belongs to the **Sun**, while the private and skillfully crafted belongs more to **Mercury**. 

Furthermore, grave music belongs to **Jupiter** and the **Sun**; light music to **Venus**; and middle music to **Mercury**.

¶ A similar reasoning also applies to the **fixed stars**. This is indeed the general rule for the human species. But the specific rule for each individual would be to investigate which star promised what good to whom in their **nativity** <term>genitura: an astrological birth chart calculated at the moment of one's birth</term>, and to seek favor from that star rather than another. And one should not expect just any gift from each star, nor what belongs to others, but expect only what is specific to it—unless perhaps many common things [are expected] from the Sun...

...gates <note>original: "portes." This is likely the end of a word like "asportes" (to carry away) or "portas" (gates) from the preceding page.</note>: as if by a certain common leader of the heavenly beings, and similarly from Jove <note>The Roman god Jupiter, who in Renaissance astrology represented a benevolent, life-giving influence.</note>; then all worldly things likewise are influenced by the soul and spirit of the world. That this world is animated just like any animal—and much more effectively so—is proven not only by Platonic reasoning but also by the testimony of Arab astrologers <note>Ficino frequently cites thinkers like Al-Kindi and Albumasar, who blended Greek philosophy with advanced medieval astrology.</note>. There they also prove that through a specific application of our spirit to the <term>Spirit of the World: A subtle, vapor-like substance that Ficino believed connected the physical body to the immaterial soul.</term>—by means of physical art <note>natural medicine or "natural magic"</note> and sacred affection—celestial blessings are drawn into our soul and body. This indeed happens through our own spirit, which is a medium within us and is then strengthened by the spirit of the world; likewise, the rays of the stars act favorably upon our spirit (which is similar in nature to those rays), and our spirit then adapts itself to those heavenly things.

[Red pilcrow] Between the soul of the world and its body, its spirit is clearly manifest, in whose power the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, the building blocks of the physical world.</note> exist. We, indeed, are able to draw this spirit in through our own spirit. **Chapter III.**

**P**recisely because of its movement and its power of generation, the worldly body appears to be a single unit everywhere. Philosophers prove this by the fact that the world generates living things from itself in all places. Therefore, it lives through a soul that is everywhere present to it and entirely adapted to it. Consequently, between the tangible and partially perishable body of the world and its very soul (whose nature is too far distant from such a body), there exists everywhere a spirit—just as there is a spirit between the soul and body within us—provided that life is everywhere and always communicated by the soul to the body.

This bridge of spirit allows the distant, divine soul of the cosmos to influence the physical, perishable human body.</summary>

But you will ask in the meantime: if the elements and living beings generate something like themselves by their own spirit, why do stones and metals not generate, which are intermediate between elements and living beings? Because, clearly, the **spirit** in them is confined by coarser matter. Which, if it were ever properly separated <note>original: "secernatur". Referring to alchemical separation or distillation.</note> and kept secret, could generate its own likeness as a seminal power <note>original: "semiaria virtus". Such a spirit, correctly drawn from gold or something else and preserved, is what the **Arabian astrologers** <note>A reference to Islamic alchemists like Geber or Rhazes, whose works were fundamental to European alchemy.</note> call the <term>elixir<note>The 'Elixir' or 'Philosopher's Stone' was believed to be a concentrated 'spirit' capable of perfecting base metals into gold.</note></term>.

This we may call sometimes "heaven" and sometimes the **fifth essence** <note>original: "quintam essentiam". The 'quintessence' or 'aether', a celestial substance distinct from the four earthly elements (earth, water, air, fire).</note>. # ON THE LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS

...our [spirit]. This is the primary exception: that the **World Soul** does not draw this spirit from the four elements as if from its own humors <note>In medieval medicine, "humors" were the bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—that governed health and temperament.</note>, just as our own spirit is drawn from ours. Instead, the Soul procreates this spirit directly (to speak in the manner of Plato or Plotinus <note>Plotinus was a major philosopher of Neoplatonism who expanded on Plato's ideas about the soul and the cosmos.</note>) from its own generative power, as if it were swelling; and together with it, the Soul creates the stars, and immediately through the spirit, it brings forth the four elements, as if all things were contained within the power of that spirit. 

The spirit itself is a most <term>subtle body</term> <note>A "subtle body" refers to a substance that is material but so refined and thin that it acts as a bridge between matter and pure spirit.</note>: almost not a body at all, and almost already soul. Likewise, it is almost not soul, and almost already a body. Within its power, there is very little of an earthly nature. There is more of the watery, and more still of the airy. On the other hand, there is a great abundance of the fiery and the stellar nature. According to the proportions of these degrees, the very quantities of the stars and elements have emerged. This spirit flourishes everywhere in all things as the immediate author and mover of all generation. Concerning this, the poet <note>original: "ille"—referring to Virgil in the Aeneid, Book VI, 726</note> said: "**The Spirit nourishes within.**" By its very nature, it is entirely bright, hot, moist, and life-giving, having obtained these qualities from the higher gifts of the soul. 

Iarchas <note>A legendary Indian sage and leader of the Brahmans mentioned in the biography of Apollonius of Tyana.</note> testified that **Apollonius of Tyana** <note>A famous Neopythagorean philosopher and reputed miracle-worker from the 1st century AD.</note> drank deeply of this spirit, saying: "No one should wonder, O Apollonius, that you have mastered the science of divination, since you carry so much ether <note>Ether was considered the "fifth element," the pure substance of the heavens.</note> in your soul."

## Our spirit drinks the spirit of the world through the rays of the Sun and Jupiter, insofar as it is itself solar and jovial. Chapter IV.

**H**ere, then, you will strive above all to introduce this spirit into yourself. For by this medium, you will receive certain natural benefits: both from the body of the world, from the soul, and even from the stars; and...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "spirit"—a subtle, vaporous substance in the body that Marsilio Ficino believes connects the physical corpse to the immortal soul. Having previously discussed how to keep this spirit pure, the text now focuses on how to make it "solar" to attract the health-giving powers of the Sun.</meta>

### [¶] ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS <note>Original: "DE VITA CAELITUS." This is likely from Marsilio Ficino’s *Three Books on Life*, specifically Book III, which deals with drawing down planetary influences.</note>

...and it should be both thin and strong at the same time. Finally, the spirit will become most heavenly—as far as our present reasoning dictates—if the rays and influence of the sun, which dominates among the heavenly bodies, are applied to it above all else. And so, from this spirit, acting as a medium <note>original: "medio"; Ficino views the spirit as the "glue" or channel between the material and the divine</note> within us, heavenly benefits will first be implanted in the spirit itself and then overflow into both our body and our soul. All heavenly benefits, I say. For all things are contained within the sun.

The sun will specifically contribute to making the spirit "solar" when it is positioned in the signs of Aries or Leo, with the moon beholding it <note>original: "aspiciente," an astrological term meaning the moon is in a specific geometric relationship or "aspect" with the sun</note>, especially while in Leo. There, the sun so enlivens our spirit that it fortifies it to repel the poison of an epidemic <note>original: "epidemie"; likely referring to the plague, which was a constant threat during the Renaissance</note>. This is clearly seen in Babylon and Egypt, and in regions that look toward Leo; there, when the sun enters the sign of Leo, it calms the epidemic, precisely for the reason we have stated. 

At that time, therefore, you should also compose various exquisite solar remedies <note>original: "solaria"; these were medicines, amulets, or dietary items associated with the sun's power</note> everywhere. Begin then to use these solar things, yet with the caution that you diligently avoid becoming dried out by the heat.

[¶] However, the spirit cannot easily become "solar" unless there is a great deal of it. For abundance <note>original: "amplitudo"</note> pertains most of all to the sun. You will create this abundance through diligence, fostering the heart with "cordial" <note>Remedies or foods meant specifically to strengthen the heart, the "solar" organ of the body</note> things both internally and externally, and also through a diet of foods that are subtle <note>original: "subtilibus"; meaning light, refined, or easily digested</note> yet highly, easily, and healthily nourishing. Frequent and light movement, timely rest, and air that is thin and serene—kept away from both extreme heat and frost—are also necessary. Above all, one must maintain a joyful mind.

->[Library stamp: LIBRARY OF THE ARSENAL]<-

<summary>This page describes how to strengthen the human spirit by aligning it with the Sun's influence, particularly when the Sun is in Aries or Leo. --- Page 46 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of cultivating a "solar" spirit, a concept in Renaissance medicine and magic where an individual aligns their internal vital spirit with the qualities of the Sun to achieve health and wisdom.</meta>

Again, the spirit will not be solar unless it is warm, subtle, and clear. You will make it subtle and clear if you avoid sad, coarse, and dark things. You will use bright and joyful things, both within and without. You will receive a mixture of light by day and night. You will cast out filth, idleness, and lethargy. Above all, you will avoid darkness. 

However, when leading the spirit <note>The "spiritus" was considered a thin, vaporous substance in the body that connected the physical soul to the material body.</note> toward the natural heat of the sun: beware lest you lead it to the third degree of heat and dryness. For solar heat itself does not naturally dry things out; otherwise, the sun would not be the lord and author of life, generation, and growth. In truth, drying only happens when its rays are trapped in the hollows of dry matter. 

Therefore, you will apply and preserve a subtle moisture in the spirit—such as is solar, and especially Jovial <note>original: "ioui⁹" / Jovian; relating to the planet Jupiter, considered the "Greater Benefic" in astrology.</note>—through the use of things of that kind, if you intend to become solar. Otherwise, you might perhaps lead it toward a Martial <note>original: "martiū" / relating to Mars, the planet of war, associated with excessive, destructive heat and dehydration.</note> quality rather than a solar one. 

Indeed, they say that Mars is similar to the Sun in only a few ways, and those are the obvious ones that are not particularly excellent; meanwhile, he is known to be an enemy. But we know that Jupiter is most similar to the Sun in the most excellent gifts—though these are more hidden—and is most friendly. 

Hence Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century author of the *Tetrabiblos*, the foundational text of Western astrology.</note>, where he discusses harmony, says that Jupiter harmonizes most perfectly with the Sun above all others, and Venus with the Moon. And all astrologers attribute universal beneficence <note>The quality of being helpful or lucky in an astrological sense.</note> to the Sun just as they do to Jupiter. Although the Sun acts on these same things more effectively, Jupiter acts under the power of the Sun. In both, heat flourishes and overcomes moisture. But in Jupiter it overcomes it moderately; in the Sun it overcomes it [exceedingly...] <margin><unclear>Fragmented notes regarding the qualities of the planets</unclear></margin>

<summary>This page explains how to refine the human spirit to a 'solar' state by avoiding darkness and lethargy, while warning against the excessive heat of Mars in favor of the balanced, life-giving heat shared by the Sun and Jupiter.</summary>

...beneficently in both ways. This is especially effective if you both compose these things together and apply them to the spirit when Jupiter regards the Sun with a <term>trine aspect|An astrological relationship where two planets are 120 degrees apart (four zodiac signs), considered very harmonious</term>, or a <term>sextile aspect|An astrological relationship where two planets are 60 degrees apart (two zodiac signs), considered helpful or compatible</term>, or at least when the Moon proceeds from an aspect with one to looking at the other. However, you will create a specifically solar or Jovian spirit separately when you have observed the Moon's aspect to either the Sun or to Jupiter. For if you have attained the nature of one, you will soon easily attain the nature of the other.

<meta>This page continues a discussion on the celestial influences of the planets, specifically focusing on the harmonious relationship between the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus, often personified as the Three Graces in Renaissance Neoplatonic thought.</meta>

...is most friendly to Jupiter, just as Jupiter is to the Sun. They are like the three <term>Graces<note>The Charites or Graces—traditionally Aglaea (Splendor), Euphrosyne (Mirth), and Thalia (Abundance)—were used by Renaissance philosophers like Marsilio Ficino to describe the harmonious flow of divine energy between the planets.</note></term> in harmony and joined with one another. From these three Graces of the heavens, and from stars of the same kind, astrologers both hope for and diligently seek favors <note>original: "gratias". The author is playing on the double meaning of "graces" as both goddesses and as favors or spiritual gifts.</note>; they believe these are transmitted through Mercury and the Moon, as if through messengers. They take care with this, though it happens easily and most commonly through the Moon. Indeed, they consider the Moon joined with Jupiter or Venus to be more fortunate than if it viewed them through a <term>sextile<note>An astrological aspect where two planets are 60 degrees apart, considered a position of harmony and opportunity.</note></term> or <term>trine<note>An astrological aspect where two planets are 120 degrees apart, considered the most auspicious and harmonious relationship between planets.</note></term> aspect. 

<margin>...dignity... to be understood... by the name... of Venus... with, and... to itself... truly... move... maximum... arises... parts... home... force from... twenty... balance... D... by which... joined... Jupiter... through... witness... nature... the same... not... either</margin>

Nevertheless, if the Moon is viewing through a trine and is also <term>received<note>original: "suscipiatur". In astrology, "reception" occurs when a planet is located in a zodiac sign ruled by the planet it is aspecting, creating a hospitable "hosting" relationship that strengthens the influence.</note></term> by them, they regard it as if it were a direct conjunction. Similarly, if it is observed by the Sun and simultaneously received. However, if we wished to run through all the powers and effects of these three stars and similar ones individually, we would be undertaking a long task: difficult to research, and most difficult to observe. 

If we turn ourselves specifically to Venus, we do not easily have the Sun. If specifically to the Sun, we do not easily have Venus. Therefore, so that we may embrace the three Graces together in one, we shall finally take refuge in Jupiter: who is intermediate in nature and effect between the Sun and Venus, very <term>tempered<note>Jupiter was considered the "Great Benefic" because he perfectly balanced the heat of the Sun and the moisture of Venus, making his influence ideal for human health and spirit.</note></term> in quality, and delivering in his own way whatever is hoped for from Venus or the Sun. He does so more magnificently and honorably than Venus, and more temperately than the Sun, and is in all things most compatible with human nature. | We shall therefore use Jovian things <note>Materials, rituals, or times associated with the planet Jupiter.</note> when both he and the Moon are worthy...

...[both] natural and accidental <note>In astrology, "natural" dignity refers to a planet's inherent strength in a zodiac sign, while "accidental" refers to its position in the houses or its speed.</note> dignity, and are in conjunction; or they regard each other favorably. And wherever she may be, let her hold one of those **twenty-eight mansions** <note>The Lunar Mansions are 28 divisions of the sky through which the Moon passes, each used for specific magical or medical purposes.</note> that is suitable both to herself and to the work being performed.

[red paragraph mark] **On the natural, vital, and animal power within us. And through which planets they are helped, and how by the aspect of the Moon to the Sun and Venus: but especially to Jupiter. Chapter VI.**

**P**articular discipline is required to correctly understand which spirit, which power, and which thing these planets especially signify. The Moon and Venus, therefore, signify the natural and generative power and spirit; Jupiter signifies the same things that increase this power, but he acts more effectively upon the liver and the stomach. He also plays no small part in the heart and the spirit, and in the **vital power** <note>original: *uirtute uitali*; the power centered in the heart that sustains life through breath and pulse.</note>: insofar as, by his own nature, he is in agreement with the Sun.

indeed and through himself. Otherwise, the heart would not properly receive the **vital spirit** <note>In traditional physiology, the 'vital spirit' was a substance believed to reside in the heart and sustain life</note> in the table of Jupiter. For this reason, the Greeks call Jupiter **Life** <note>original: "Iouem gręci appellāt uitam" — a wordplay on the Greek name for Zeus (Zēn), which is similar to the word for 'to live' (zēn)</note> and "he through whom there is life." Astrologers also testify that he holds power over the **animal spirit** <note>The 'animal spirit' (from 'anima' or soul) was thought to reside in the brain and govern movement and sensation</note>, saying that Jupiter contributes to philosophy, the discovery of truth, and religion. And Plato, where he says that philosophers originate from Jupiter. Homer also signified this, following the opinion of the ancients, saying: 

-> "Such is the mind of men as the father of men and gods brings upon them each day." <- 
<note>A reference to the *Odyssey*, Book 18</note>

Truly, no other deity is named in this way except Jupiter. The **Sun** signifies especially the vital spirit and the heart; and he has no small amount—indeed, quite a bit—in the head, because of sensation and motion, of which he himself is the lord, nor does he abandon natural power. 

**Mercury** rules the brain and the instruments of the senses, and therefore the animal spirit. 

Proinde the safest path will be to do nothing without the benefit of the **Moon**. Since she commonly, frequently, and easily transmits celestial things to the lower realms, they call her "another Sun," as she creates the four seasons of the year within any given month. In her first quarter, the **Peripatetics** <note>Followers of Aristotle’s philosophy</note> believe she is hot and moist. In the second, hot and dry. In the third, cold and dry. In the fourth, cold and moist. Her light is undoubtedly the light of the Sun. She rules the **humors** <note>The four primary fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—believed to control health and temperament</note> and generation; all changes of the fetus in the womb are measured by her turnings, and whenever she is joined to the Sun, she receives a life-giving power from him.

[Decorative red mark] **ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS.**

...to receive, which it pours into the moisture; and there also from Mercury a power that mixes the <term>humors</term> <note>The four bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—which medieval and Renaissance medicine believed determined a person's health and temperament.</note>. This power Mercury brings both through his transformation into all his own things and through his multiple rotations. There also, soon after, a power from Venus which leads to forms suitable for procreation. It will indeed be worthwhile to remember that the Moon's daily course is divided into four quarters. In the first, as it ascends from the east to the middle of the sky, it increases moisture and the <term>natural spirit</term> <note>original: "spiritum naturalem"; the subtle vapor believed to arise in the liver to govern nutrition and growth.</note>. In the second, as it heads from the middle of the sky toward its setting, it produces the opposite in us. The third goes from the setting to the point below the middle of the sky; and again it increases that spirit and moisture. The fourth falls from there toward the rising, and diminishes them in turn. This appears most clearly on the shores of the ocean, where the sea more obviously approaches and recedes according to this course; and in the same order, vigor changes in those who are sick. It is also probable that the Sun, through these same quarters of its own, increases or diminishes natural heat and the <term>vital spirit</term> <note>original: "spiritum vitalem"; the spirit centered in the heart that regulates life-force and heat throughout the body.</note>. Also the <term>animal spirit</term> <note>original: "animalem [spiritum]"; the spirit centered in the brain responsible for sensation, thought, and movement.</note>, insofar as it has Mercury as a companion. Once these things are known, a physician will be able to observe more opportune times for restoring the humor, natural heat, and any spirit. But enough of the Moon for now.

Nor is it fitting to dismiss Jupiter, in whose second month <note>referring to the traditional astrological belief that different planets govern the fetus during different months of pregnancy; Jupiter was often associated with the second and ninth months.</note> we received life, and in whose other month we were more commonly and happily born. He is one who, in quality and effect, stands between the Sun and Venus, and also between the Sun and the Moon.

...middle: and therefore it embraces all things. Truly, we consider it a sacrilege and a danger to overlook the Sun himself, the lord of the heavens—unless perhaps someone says that he who possesses Jupiter <note>In Renaissance astrology, Jupiter was considered the "Great Benefic," a planet of health, growth, and justice.</note> already possesses the Sun within Jupiter, for in that place it is most perfectly <term>tempered</term> <note>To be "tempered" means to have qualities balanced in a way that is healthy and sustainable for human life, rather than being too extreme, like the Sun’s raw heat.</note> for human beings.

Certainly, not only Iamblichus <note>Iamblichus (c. 245–325 AD) was a Syrian philosopher who taught that divine rituals could help the soul ascend.</note> and Julian <note>The Emperor Julian "the Apostate" (331–363 AD), who wrote a famous hymn to the Sun as a spiritual king.</note>, but everyone affirms that all the <term>virtues</term> <note>In this context, "virtues" refers to the specific spiritual powers or healing properties of the stars.</note> of the heavenly bodies exist within the Sun. And Proclus <note>Proclus (412–485 AD) was a major Neoplatonic philosopher who wrote extensively on how celestial bodies influence the world below.</note> says that at the sight of the Sun, all the powers of all the celestial bodies are gathered and unified into one. 

No one will deny that Jupiter is, in a sense, a Sun tempered specifically for us. Likewise, do not neglect the Moon when she is tempered toward Venus. For this greatly aids in a healthy and prosperous life. Since Venus makes a person fertile and joyful, you should observe her for this reason. Although if you rightly combine the Moon (which is similar to Venus—that is, entirely equal in moisture and only slightly less warm) with Jupiter or the Sun, you already possess nearly the same influence as Venus.

What then? So that you may proceed by the safest and most comfortable path of all, observe the Moon when she gazes upon the Sun and joins with Jupiter <note>This refers to an "aspect" or "conjunction," where planets reach specific geometric relationships in the sky that were thought to beam combined energy to Earth.</note>—or at least when she gazes at both Jupiter and the Sun at once. Or certainly, observe when, after looking at the Sun, she moves quickly toward a meeting or a gaze with Jupiter. 

At that very time, combine these influences, or apply to yourself things related to the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus simultaneously. But if necessity or business forces you to seek refuge in only one of the great powers, flee to Jupiter himself, or better yet, to the Moon and Jupiter together. For no star strengthens our natural powers—indeed, all of them—more than Jup[iter]... <note>The text cuts off mid-word; it is clearly continuing to discuss Jupiter's superior influence on human health.</note>

...Jupiter, nourishes and strengthens. No other planet, in turn, promises more prosperous things: both more consistent and more abundant. And to receive his influence is fortunate everywhere. Taking in the Sun, however, is perhaps not safe everywhere. For the Sun <note>original: "ille" referring to Jupiter / "hic" referring to the Sun</note> always helps, while the Sun often seems to cause harm. Venus, moreover, is as if weak. Therefore, Jupiter alone is called the "Helping Father" <note>The name Jupiter is derived from the archaic Latin "Iuvans Pater," or Helping Father.</note>. This is confirmed by Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century mathematician and astronomer whose works were foundational to Renaissance science.</note> where he says that a medicine <term>medicine (pharmacun)</term> hardly moves a person's nature at all, except when the Moon is in alignment with Jupiter. To such a degree does he believe that the entire nature of the whole body is properly strengthened from that source. 

I have also experienced that even when the Moon is joined with Venus, medicine hardly has any effect. Although when we greatly fear <term>phlegm (pituita)</term>, we should observe the Moon especially in relation to the Sun; but when we fear bile and the resulting dryness, we look to Venus. Nevertheless, the direction of the Moon toward Jupiter is helpful for all these conditions in some way, and especially for expelling <term>black bile (atra bilis)</term> <note>Associated with melancholy and depression in Renaissance medicine.</note>, as well as for restoring and strengthening the common <term>complexion (constitution)</term> of human beings. 

For just as licorice and rose oil warm things that are too cold and cool things that are too warm—and wine acts similarly—and furthermore moistens what is dry and dries what is too humid; so too is Jupiter agreeable to human heat, just like wine, rose oil, chamomile, and licorice. Therefore, when you hear Abu Ma'shar <note>Abu Ma'shar (787–886 AD), known in the West as Albumasar, was a preeminent Persian astrologer.</note> saying: "There is no life for living things, besides God, except through the Sun and Moon," understand this in terms of the <term>influx (celestial influence)</term> that pertains to all living things in common. But the more specific and most suitable influence for humans comes from Jupiter. There are, however, in nature...

the body’s powers of attracting, retaining, digesting <note>original Latin: "coquendi," literally "cooking." In ancient medicine, digestion was viewed as a heat-based process of refining food</note>, and expelling. Therefore, Jupiter himself aids all of these. He especially assists the power of digesting—or rather, the refining virtue—as well as the powers of generating, nourishing, and increasing growth. This is due to his "airy" and abundant moisture, and his great heat, which holds a moderate dominance over that moisture. 

Truly, it is through the rays of Jupiter, which are spread everywhere at all times, that the Sun’s own light is most perfectly tempered <note>In this context, "tempered" means balanced or diluted to a safe, healthy level</note> for the health of human beings; meanwhile, the rays of Venus constantly contribute to this same effect, and the Moon transfers these influences in a similar way. The rays of Venus and the Moon, being somewhat more "moist," require a certain balancing influence; just as the rays of the Sun, being hotter, require the tempering of a certain moisture. 

The rays of Jupiter, however, require no such balancing. For what else is Jupiter but a kind of Sun, tempered from the very beginning for the welfare of things—especially human affairs? Or what else is he but a Moon or a Venus made hotter and more powerful? Therefore, astrologers look to Jupiter as a sign of a fertile, clear, and healthy year, and they hope for remedies from him for looming diseases. And when Empedocles <note>A Greek pre-Socratic philosopher who proposed the theory of the four elements</note> assigned specific duties to each of the planets, he named Jupiter alone as the prince of generation, imitating Orpheus <note>A legendary figure in Greek myth, often associated with sacred hymns and the origins of life</note> in this regard.

<meta>This page continues a discussion on the optimal astrological conditions for the Moon when performing "the desired work," likely the creation of talismans or medical remedies, as described by Marsilio Ficino in his third book of "On Life."</meta>

# ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE

...and with an appearance suitable for the desired work. <note>The "desired work" refers to the creation of an astrological talisman or the timing of a medical treatment.</note> Let it [the Moon] not be on the ecliptic, nor under the rays of the Sun <note>original: "sub radiis Solis." This refers to "combustion," where a planet is too close to the Sun's heat to be effective.</note> within twelve degrees on either side, unless by chance it is in the same minute with the Sun. Most people, however, wish all planets to be strong when they are in "unity" <term>cazimi; literally "in the heart of the Sun"</term> with the Sun. Indeed, they measure "unity" as thirty-two minutes, so that sixteen are counted before the Sun and sixteen after.

Let it [the Moon] not be impeded by Saturn or Mars; let it not descend in southern latitude when it leaves the twelve aforementioned degrees. Let it not be opposite the Sun, nor diminished in light, nor slow in its course—namely, when it does not travel twelve degrees in one day. Let it not be in the <term>via combusta</term> <note>The "Burned Path," a section of the zodiac considered unlucky by ancient astrologers.</note> from the 28th degree of Libra to the 3rd degree of Scorpio; nor in the eighth house; nor in the ascendant; nor in the "bounds" <term>termini; specific subdivisions of zodiac signs</term> of Mars or Saturn. 

Some, however, do not want the Moon in the sixth, twelfth, ninth, or fourth houses. In the other regions of the heavens, they approve of it. Where you cannot encompass all these conditions, at least look for Jupiter or Venus in the ascendant or the tenth house. For in this way, they remedy the weaknesses of the Moon. 

And it would not be out of place to remember that as the Moon increases in light, so not only moisture, but also spirit <note>Renaissance medicine viewed "spirit" as a subtle vapor that linked the soul to the body.</note> and virtue are increased for us; and these are expanded toward the surface of the body. This is especially true in its second quarter. When it decreases, however, the opposite happens, especially in the last quarter. Its first trine aspect to the Sun is thoroughly beneficial. This is followed by the first sextile, and then the second sextile. Just as the Moon is filled with light, so it is filled with heat.

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues a discourse on medical astrology, specifically focusing on how the phases and planetary aspects of the Moon influence the four "faculties" of the human body (attraction, retention, digestion, and expulsion).</meta>

Some, therefore, seem not so much to observe where the Moon looks at the Sun—for she always looks at him—but rather when she has the most light, especially while she is increasing <note>waxing</note>. Truly, let her look meanwhile in a **trine** or **sextile** <note>beneficial astrological aspects where planets are 120 or 60 degrees apart</note> aspect at **Jupiter** or **Venus**.

Accordingly, the fiery signs help the **attractive faculty**. The earthy signs help the **retentive**. The airy help the **digestive**. The watery help the **expulsive** <note>In traditional medicine, these four "faculties" governed how the body took in nutrients, kept them, processed them, and removed waste</note>. 

If you wish to aid all of these within yourself: you will especially strengthen the **attractive** faculty through fiery things, when the Moon, positioned in fiery signs or stations, looks at Jupiter—namely, **Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius**. 

You will strengthen the **retentive** faculty through earthy things: most powerfully when she [the Moon] gazes at the same [Jupiter] while located in earthy signs or stations: **Taurus, Virgo, or Capricorn**. 

You will strengthen the **digestive and generative** faculties through airy things—namely, **Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius**—whenever she looks up at or enters the sign of Jupiter under the airy signs. 

You will strengthen the **expulsive** faculty through the watery: **Cancer, Pisces, and Scorpio**, when she herself, placed under watery signs, is illuminated by the rays of Jupiter. 

Most especially in all these things you will achieve your desires if Jupiter occupies the same or similar, or at least not entirely dissimilar, signs or mansions. 

If you are about to stimulate the bowels with solid medicines, take [the Moon in] **Pisces**. With liquid medicines, **Scorpio**. With moderate ones, **Cancer**. If you are about to be purged through the lower parts, choose **Pisces and Scorpio**. Through the upper parts <note>such as by vomiting</note>, choose **Cancer**. 

You shall avoid the evil aspect of **Saturn** or **Mars** toward the Moon. For the former <note>Saturn</note> vexes the stomach; the latter <note>Mars</note> dissolves <note>causes distress or diarrhea in</note> the intestines. You shall avoid **Capricorn** and **Taurus**, for they bring about nausea. You know that a limb is not to be provoked when...

->**CONCERNING LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS.**<-

¶ When the Moon occupies a zodiac sign ruling over a fully formed limb, it moves the <term>humors</term> <note>The four bodily fluids (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile) believed to determine health and temperament.</note>. However, it is better that they be nourished. Let these things we have said concerning the power and the <term>natural spirit</term> <note>original: "spiritu naturali"; in Renaissance medicine, this was the "breath" or energy residing in the liver that governed nutrition, growth, and metabolism.</note> ruling primarily in the liver—which is divided into the four functions we described—be sufficient.

Now, what do we advise regarding the power and <term>vital spirit</term> <note>original: "spirituque uitali"; the energy residing in the heart that governs circulation, heat, and life force.</note> flourishing in the heart? This has already been sufficiently indicated for the most part. For these things are aided especially by fiery influences and, in a certain way, by similar airy ones, when the Moon—residing in similar astrological <term>houses or mansions</term> <note>Specific divisions of the zodiac used in astrology to determine the strength and nature of a planet's influence.</note>—looks toward Jupiter; especially if it "embraces" <note>An astrological term for a close conjunction or aspect.</note> both Jupiter and the Sun.

You can also strengthen the <term>animal spirit</term> <note>original: "virtutem animalem"; the spirit residing in the brain that governs sensation, thought, and voluntary motion.</note>—which rules the head through sense and motion—primarily through airy influences, with fiery ones joined to them, when the Moon looks toward Jupiter in similar houses or seats; especially if it embraces Jupiter almost entirely, as well as Mercury.

¶ Here I truly warn you not to think that Mercury is watery or earthy, a thing which I myself once suspected.

¶ Otherwise, it would not contribute to the movements and the speed of the <term>intellect</term> <note>original: "ingenii"; referring to the mind's natural capacity, wit, or genius.</note>. But you should know that it is, in a certain way, airy. For this same reason, it is so mobile and so easily changeable; it contributes so much to the intellect, especially when it is positioned in Aquarius, which is a highly airy sign. Indeed, the moisture in Mercury is tempered, and its heat is slight. When positioned under the Sun <note>This refers to Mercury being "combust" or in close conjunction with the Sun.</note>, it is said to dry things out. However, when it is further from the Sun, it is thought to moisten. In the first case, it heats greatly from the nature of the Sun; in the second, it heats very little and moistens more by its own nature. By the heat of...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological medicine and the practical application of celestial influences on the human body, specifically focusing on the timing of treatments relative to the Moon and the planets.</meta>

# ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE

**J**ust as we have said, you will be able to nurture the entire body; the head especially if you observe the planets in **Aries**, or each in its first <term>domicile</term><note>A 'domicile' or 'house' is the zodiac sign over which a planet has its primary influence; for example, Aries is the domicile of Mars.</note>. The heart and diaphragm if in **Leo**. The stomach and liver if in **Cancer** and **Sagittarius**, or at least in **Virgo**. And you receive those things which specifically seek out the limb in question. It is also beneficial to know which organs each planet governs in any given sign. You can also provide specific counsel for each person according to the distinction of their age through the four <term>ages of the Moon</term>.

For the Moon is "young" from the new moon until the first <term>quadrature</term><note>The 'quadrature' refers to the moon being at a 90-degree angle (square) from the sun; here it refers to the first quarter phase.</note>. Then, until the full moon, she is youthful and in her prime. From there to the second quadrature, she is both in her prime and aging. From then until the conjunction <note>The 'conjunction' here refers to the Moon returning to the same position as the Sun, marking the new moon.</note>, she is old. Therefore, you shall successfully apply the age of the moon to the age of the body being treated. 

This is especially effective if you then receive her <term>aspect</term><note>An 'aspect' is the angular relationship between celestial bodies that astrologers believe transmits influence.</note> toward any of the **Three Graces** <note>Ficino identifies the 'Three Graces' as the planets Jupiter, Venus, and the Sun, which he considers the most beneficial and 'gracious' influences for health.</note>. Indeed, such an aspect of the Moon always and quickly bestows gracious gifts. Yet these gifts are not easily long-lasting or greatest, unless—beyond the gaze of the moon—the Graces themselves are looking at one another, or are almost about to look at one another, whether all three or just two of them. 

In particular, the <term>fixed signs</term><note>Fixed signs—Leo, Aquarius, Taurus, and Scorpio—fall in the middle of seasons and were thought to represent stability and duration.</note> provide longevity. Namely: **Leo**, **Aquarius**, **Taurus**, and **Scorpio**. If by chance you cannot presently direct the moon to the "gracious" planets at the right time, choose fixed stars possessing the nature of the Graces—that is, stars having the nature of **Jupiter**, **Venus**, or the **Sun**—and choose the moon directed toward them. It is safer, however, for the moon to be approaching Jupiter or Venus in the meantime...

<meta>This page continues a discussion on the use of celestial talismans and astrological medicine, specifically focusing on the use of rings. It transitions from the historical anecdotes of Apollonius of Tyana to a theoretical explanation of how such objects affect the human 'spirit' and concludes by citing Thomas Aquinas as an authority.</meta>

->### ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS.<-

...greatly esteemed them. For Damis and Philostratus <note>Damis was the companion of Apollonius of Tyana; Philostratus was the 3rd-century author who wrote Apollonius's biography.</note> recount that Iarchas, the leader of the Indian sages, fashioned seven rings by some similar reasoning, named after the names of the seven stars <note>In the 15th century, the "seven stars" referred to the planets: the Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note>: and gave them to Apollonius of Tyana. He thereafter wore them one by one on individual days, according to the names of the days, distinguishing between them. Indeed, Iarchas told Apollonius that his own grandfather, a philosopher, had lived for one hundred and thirty years, perhaps relying on a celestial gift of this kind. Apollonius also used these rings thereafter, and even in his hundredth year, so they say, he surpassed younger men in vigor.

Finally, if rings of this kind have any power from on high, I judge that it pertains not so much to the soul or to the gross <note>In this context, "gross" means the dense, physical matter of the body.</note> body as to the <term>spirit|The "spiritus" was believed to be a very subtle, vapor-like substance that acted as a bridge, connecting the physical body to the immaterial soul.</term>: for when the ring is gradually warmed by the skin, it is affected in this way or that, so that the spirit is made firmer, or brighter, more intense, or gentler, more serious, or more joyful. These conditions indeed pass entirely into the body, and in a certain way into the sensing soul, which usually indulges the body. 

But regarding those who promise that rings will be useful against <term>demons|Here referring to malevolent spirits or harmful astrological influences.</term> or enemies, or for the favor of princes: this is either a fiction, or it is derived from the fact that they make the spirit fearless and firm, or even gentle, lovable through obedience, and graceful.

Now indeed, if I were to say that celestial things contribute to talent, art, and fortune beyond mere physical health, I would not disagree with our **Thomas Aquinas**: who, in the third book of his *Against the Gentiles* <note>original: "contra gentiles"; referring to the *Summa contra Gentiles*, a major work of the 13th-century theologian Thomas Aquinas.</note>, proves that from celestial bodies...

<summary>Ficino discusses the legendary astrological rings of Apollonius of Tyana, arguing that such talismans work by refining the "spirit" (the link between body and soul) rather than by magic, and aligns his views with Thomas Aquinas.</summary>
<keywords>Apollonius of Tyana, Iarchas, astrological rings, spiritus, Thomas Aquinas, planetary medicine, longevity</keywords>

--- Page 64 ---
...something of the first principles <note>original: "primi"; likely referring to the primary celestial causes or planetary influences discussed on the preceding page</note> exists within our body. By their gift, we are often inclined to choose what is best, even when we do not understand the reason or the ultimate goal. In this regard, he <note>The author is likely referencing a commentator on Aristotle or a Neoplatonic philosopher like Plotinus</note> calls such people "the well-fortuned," and—with Aristotle in agreement—the "well-born" <note>original: "bene natos"</note>. He adds also that through celestial virtue <term>celestial virtue: the specific power or "influence" transmitted from the stars and planets that affects physical matter and human aptitude</term>, some people become effective in the results of certain arts (to use his own words), such as a soldier successful in conquering, a farmer in planting, or a doctor in healing. For he says: just as herbs and stones possess certain marvelous powers from the heavens that go beyond their basic elemental nature, so too do certain men possess such powers in the arts.

For my part, I will be satisfied if celestial influences contribute in any way—as if through medicines, whether internal or external—to prosperous health; provided that, while we seek the health of the body, we do not suffer any loss of the health of the soul.

¶ Let us attempt nothing at all that is prohibited by holy religion. <note>This reflects the author's frequent "disclaimer" that his interest in astrology and natural magic remains strictly within the bounds of Christian orthodoxy.</note>

<meta>This page continues a technical discussion of planetary dignities and the division of the zodiac signs into "terms," likely following the "Egyptian" system common in Renaissance astrology. It follows a previous section that would have introduced Mars's dignities.</meta>

### ON THE HEAVENLY LIFE
<note>original title: *DE VITA CAELITVS*</note>

...Mars’s <note>The text begins with the suffix "-tis," the end of the Latin *Martis* (Mars)</note> domicile is Scorpio and Aries. Its <term>exaltation</term> <note>A sign where a planet's influence is particularly strengthened or "exalted."</note> is Capricorn. The seat of the Sun indeed is Leo. Its kingdom <note>original: *Regnum*, here used as a synonym for exaltation</note> is Aries.

The dwelling-place of Venus is Taurus and Libra. Its exaltation is Pisces. The house of Mercury is Virgo and Gemini. Its kingdom is Virgo. The home of the Moon is Cancer. Its exaltation is Taurus. Saturn and Jupiter have <term>triplicity</term> <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (fire, air, water, or earth).</note> in the fiery and airy signs. The Sun in the fiery only. Mercury in the airy only. Mars, Venus, and the Moon in the watery and earthy signs. Any of the planets except the Sun and Moon possess certain boundaries of their own in any sign: which we also call <term>terms</term> <note>original: *terminos*; these are specific degree-segments within a zodiac sign assigned to a particular planet, granting it minor dignity.</note>.

Therefore, in **Aries**, Jupiter holds the first six terms there. Venus the six following. Mercury the next eight. Mars five thereafter, and Saturn the last five.

In **Taurus**, in a similar order thereafter, Venus has eight boundaries. Mercury six; Jupiter eight; Saturn five; and finally Mars three.

In **Gemini**, Mercury has six; Jupiter six; Venus five; Mars seven; and Saturn six.

In **Cancer**, Mars has seven; Venus six; Mercury the same number <note>original: *totidem*</note>; Jupiter seven; and Saturn four.

In **Leo**, Jupiter has six; Venus five; Saturn seven; Mercury six; and Mars the same number.

In **Virgo**, Mercury has seven; Venus ten; Jupiter four; Mars seven; and Saturn two.

In **Libra**, Saturn has six; Mercury eight; Jupiter seven; Venus the same number; and Mars two.

In **Scorpio**, Mars has seven; Venus four...
<margin>l.i.</margin>

Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer. Therefore, they think that the Sun and Moon 
have in these signs that lordship and effect which the other 
planets have in their own bounds. Besides bounds, 
the planets have their own **faces** in the signs, which the Greeks call 
**Decans** <note>original: *Decanos*; from the Greek word for "ten," referring to ten-degree subdivisions of a zodiac sign</note>, each occupying ten degrees of a sign. In Aries: the 
first face is of Mars; the second of the Sun, who follows Mars 
in the heavens. According to the **order of the Chaldeans** <note>The Chaldean order arranges the planets by their relative speed as seen from Earth: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon</note>, the third is of Venus, who succeeds 
the Sun in the heavens. In Taurus: the first face is of Mercury, 
following Venus; the second of the Moon, who succeeds 
Mercury; the third of Saturn. For one must return to him [Saturn], 
the number of planets being now completed. In Gemini: the first of 
Jupiter, who follows Saturn; the second of Mars; 
the third of the Sun, in the same order and similarly thereafter.

->### ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE.<-

¶ How we ought to use the planets in medicines. Chapter X.

**W**e have indeed recounted what dignities <note>original: "dignitates"; specific positions in the zodiac, like 'house' or 'exaltation,' where a planet's power is most effective</note> the planets possess within the signs, so that whenever we are preparing or compounding remedies that pertain to a specific planet, we may know how to position that planet within its own dignities. This is especially important when a planet holds the principal role in our annual revolution <note>the "solar return" or the astrological chart for the current year</note> and our birth chart <term>geneſi: the nativity or horoscope at the moment of birth</term>. In such cases, even Saturn and Mars—which we should otherwise suppress—must instead be elevated by us if they happen to be the signifiers of our birth <note>In traditional astrology, Saturn and Mars are considered "malefic" or harmful, but if they rule a person's birth chart, they must be strengthened to ensure the person's well-being</note>.

¶ We shall truly find this account most worthwhile if, when we intend to use the benefits of the Moon, Venus, and Jupiter in compounding medicines, we take care that they are not located within the "bounds" <term>finibus: also known as "terms," these are specific subdivisions of each zodiac sign governed by different planets</term> of Saturn or Mars. The exception is when we are perhaps compelled by necessity: either to use Saturn to restrain a physical dissolution and repress a fever <note>original: "eſtum," meaning a burning heat or inflammation</note>, or to use Mars to warm those things that are freezing or sluggish...

<summary>Ficino explains the importance of timing medical compositions according to the "dignities" and "bounds" of the planets, noting that even traditionally harmful planets like Saturn and Mars must be strengthened if they rule an individual's birth chart.</summary>
<keywords>Marsilio Ficino, medical astrology, planetary dignities, Saturn, Mars, birth chart, zodiacal bounds, humoral medicine</keywords>

...the tenth house is completed. The eleventh follows it. 
But the twelfth <term>falls away</term><note>This refers to a 'cadent' house, which was traditionally thought to weaken the influence of a planet.</note> from the <term>ascendant</term><note>The eastern point of the zodiac rising on the horizon at a specific time.</note>. ¶ Therefore, so that the planets may be powerful, they must be held in the <term>angles</term><note>The four most powerful points in a chart: the East (Ascendant), West (Descendant), and the Midheaven/Lower Heaven.</note>—either of the East, the West, or the Midheaven in either direction. Indeed, they are most powerful in the angle of the ascendant, or in the tenth house, which holds the Midheaven directly above one's head, or at least in those regions that immediately follow the angles. Although, they believe the Sun truly <term>rejoices</term><note>A term indicating a planet is in a favorable or comfortable house.</note> in the ninth house, which falls from the Midheaven; while the Moon rejoices in the third house, which is also falling. ¶ Astrologers want two rules to be kept in mind regarding these matters. One indeed concerns the nature of the illness; the other concerns the doctor. Surely, where the seventh house of the sick person is made <term>unfortunate</term><note>Afflicted by 'malefic' planets like Saturn or Mars, suggesting a poor outcome.</note> by Saturn or Mars, or if its <term>lord</term><note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign located on the house's threshold.</note> is unhappy: separate the doctor from the sick person, if you believe <term>Ptolemy</term><note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century scholar whose 'Tetrabiblos' was the foundational text for Western astrology.</note>. ¶ Furthermore, when choosing a doctor, they command you to avoid one who is <term>Saturnian</term><note>Cold, slow, or melancholic in temperament.</note> or <term>Martial</term><note>Aggressive or rash in temperament.</note>. Instead, seek one in whose <term>birth chart</term><note>The 'genitura' or natal horoscope.</note> the sixth house has been made fortunate through an <term>aspect</term><note>The geometric angle between planets.</note> of the Sun, or through Venus or Jupiter, in whatever way that may be. We say a sign or planet is "unfortunate" because of Saturn or Mars—unless these are in their own homes or kingdoms—specifically when they are either present there or looking upon that place through an <term>opposite</term> or <term>square</term> aspect. We call it an "opposite" aspect between those points which are separated by the longest possible interval. Truly, it is a "square" aspect when one is distant from the other by a fourth part of the sky, that is, by a space of three signs. However, Saturn and Mars clearly [harm] less through a conjunction than through an opposition or square...

...others are harmful: when they receive them like guests in their home, their kingdom, or their boundary. <note>In astrology, "domicile," "kingdom," and "term" refer to specific portions of the zodiac where a planet is thought to have varying degrees of power or essential dignity.</note> Just as the fortunate <term>planets</term> <note>The "fortunate" or "benefic" planets traditionally refer to Jupiter and Venus.</note> are more beneficial when—in addition to the <term>sextile</term> or <term>trine</term> <term>aspect</term> <note>These are angles of 60 and 120 degrees between planets, considered harmonious and helpful in astrology.</note> and the conjunction—they receive others as we have described. However, the planets fear a <term>conjunction</term> with the Sun <note>original: "Solis coniunctionem"; this refers to a planet being "combust," where its influence is thought to be overwhelmed or scorched by being too close to the Sun.</note> but rejoice in its aspect, namely the trine or sextile. Therefore, it is necessary to remember that:

**Aries** presides over the head and face; 
**Taurus** over the neck; 
**Gemini** over the arms and shoulders; 
**Cancer** over the chest, lungs, stomach, and muscles; 
**Leo** over the heart, stomach, liver, back, and the rear ribs; 
**Virgo** over the intestines and the base of the stomach; 
**Libra** over the kidneys, the upper thigh, and the buttocks; 
**Scorpio** over the genitals and the womb; 
**Sagittarius** over the thigh and the groin; 
**Capricorn** over the knees; 
**Aquarius** over the shins and lower legs; 
**Pisces** over the feet. 

¶ Mindful of this order, you shall be careful not to touch a limb with iron <note>original: "ferro"; referring to surgical knives or bloodletting</note>, or fire <note>cauterization</note>, or cupping glasses, when the Moon is passing through its corresponding sign. For at that time, the Moon increases the <term>humors</term> <note>The four vital bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—believed to govern human health and temperament.</note> in that limb. Their abundance prevents healing and burdens the vital power of the limb. During such times, you should instead take care to nourish the limb with certain "friendly" remedies, applied either externally or internally, at the proper and fortunate time. 

¶ It is truly beneficial to know which sign was <term>ascending</term> <note>The "Ascendant" or Rising Sign is the zodiac sign that was rising on the eastern horizon at the exact moment of an individual's birth.</note> at the time of your birth. For besides Aries, this sign also represents the head for you; and in this sign, the Moon watches over your head.

¶ Furthermore, when the Moon enters Aries, it is an opportune time to bathe <note>original: "bal"; likely an abbreviation for "balneare" (to bathe) or "balneum" (a bath).</note>

...neither attempt baths nor washings. When the Moon is in Cancer, bloodletting is successful. Nor should <note>Ptolemy (c. 100–170 AD) was a Greco-Egyptian mathematician and astronomer whose work formed the basis of medieval astrology.</note> Ptolemy’s precept regarding the purging of the bowels be hidden from us. If, however, the rays of Saturn or Mars <note>Saturn and Mars are the "malefics," associated with cold/restriction and heat/aggression respectively.</note> are mixed, it is the opposite. But having now wandered quite far, let us finally return to the spirit, to life, and to the graces.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Spirit of the World" (Spiritus Mundi) from Book III of Marsilio Ficino's Three Books on Life. It establishes the foundational Neoplatonic idea that the entire universe is a living organism.</meta>

-># ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE.<-
### Chapter XI: And what things pertain to each planet.

**H**ere indeed all these things tend: so that our **spirit** <note>Ficino uses the term <term>spiritus</term> to describe a fine, subtle vapor in the body that acts as a bridge between the physical flesh and the immortal soul.</note>, properly prepared and purified through natural means, may receive as much as possible from the very **spirit of the world's life** <note>The <term>spiritus mundi</term> is a cosmic "glue" that Neoplatonists believed filled the universe, transmitting life-force from the stars to the Earth.</note> by means of the rays of the stars, received at the opportune time. The life of the world is indeed implanted in all things; it clearly spreads into herbs and trees, which are like the hairs and tresses of its body. It swells, moreover, in stones and metals, like its teeth and bones. It also sprouts in living shells <note>Original: "conchas", likely referring to mollusks or crustaceans which were thought to generate spontaneously from the earth's vitality.</note> clinging to the earth and stones. For these things do not live so much by their own individual life as by that common life of the whole. 

This common life flourishes even more above the earth in subtler bodies, as if they were closer to the soul. Through its inward vigor, the water, air, and fire possess their own living things and are moved. This life warms and agitates the air and fire even more than the earth and water with perpetual motion. And finally, it enlivens the celestial bodies—as if they were the world's head, heart, or eyes—to the greatest degree. 

From there, through the stars as if through eyes, it pours out rays everywhere—not only visible rays, but also "visual" rays <note>original: "visuales". In ancient optics, eyes were thought to emit active rays that reached out to touch the objects they perceived.</note>. In the manner of an ostrich <note>original: "more struti". A reference to the medieval belief that ostriches hatch their eggs simply by gazing at them; Ficino uses this to explain how the stars influence the Earth through their "gaze."</note>, as we have said elsewhere, it gazes upon the lower things and warms them by looking; indeed, by touching them in this way, it generates, forms, and moves them in every way. 

Therefore, you will receive the motion of the world's life through the motion of glittering water, of clear air, of fire at a moderate distance, and of the heavens, if you yourself are also moved gently and in a similar fashion, performing certain rotations according to your strength.

->.l.iiii<-

<summary>Ficino explains the "Spirit of the World," arguing that the universe is a living organism where stars act as eyes that influence earthly life through their gaze. He suggests that humans can capture this celestial vitality by purifying their own spirit and mimicking the movements of the heavens.</summary>
<keywords>Marsilio Ficino, Spiritus Mundi, Celestial Influence, Neoplatonism, Astrology, Visual Rays, Natural Magic</keywords>

...avoiding dizziness: surveying the heavens with your eyes and contemplating them in your mind. Likewise, through a certain frequent use of plants and similarly living things, you can draw in a great deal from the <term>spirit of the world<note>original: "mundi spiritu." In Renaissance philosophy, this was a subtle, invisible substance that permeated the universe, acting as a bridge between the physical and the divine.</note></term>. 

You will benefit especially if you are nourished and fostered by those things while they are still living and fresh, as if they were still clinging to Mother Earth. You should spend time among sweetly-scented plants—or at least those that do not smell bad—as often as possible. For indeed, all herbs, flowers, trees, and fruits are fragrant, even if you often fail to notice it. 

By this scent, which acts like a breath and spirit of the world’s life, they refresh and enliven you from all sides. Your own <term>spirit<note>original: "spiritus." In this context, it refers to the "animal spirits" or subtle vapors within the human body that mediate between the physical organs and the immortal soul.</note></term> is very similar in nature to such scents; because this spirit is the intermediary between the body and the soul, these scents easily refresh the body as well and are wonderfully beneficial to the soul. Among these things, you should remain under the open sky for a very long time during the day, as far as it can be done safely and comfortably...

--- Page 73 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of health and the preservation of the spirit, focusing specifically on how movement and celestial influences affect human vitality.</meta>

# ->ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE<-

First, you should avoid an excessive imbalance of the air. Then, you should move more frequently during those times when, in addition to the air’s balance and clarity, the position of the stars is most beneficial to humans. Likewise, you should choose places that are more fragrant than others, through which you might change your location daily, keeping yourself in a state of almost continuous and gentle motion. I have always taught that changing one's location should be done with careful selection, because the benefits of the celestial beings and of universal nature are distributed among us in various things and places here and there; all of these, ultimately, are to be enjoyed. 

I will pass over the fact that variety prevents boredom—which is an enemy to the <term>spirits</term> <note>original: *spiritibus*; in Renaissance medicine, the "spirit" was a subtle vapor that linked the soul to the body</note> and is characteristic of <note>The planet Saturn was associated with melancholy, stagnation, and "tedium" or boredom.</note> Saturn—but variety instead brings pleasure. Through this pleasure (if I may say so), Venus herself, the friend of pleasure, enters into the spirit; and once she has entered, she immediately performs her duty of propagating it. Briefly, if anyone considers the account of Paradise and the use of the "fruit of life" in the writings of Moses, or the similar way of life proposed by Plato in the *Phaedo*, or what Pliny says about the <note>original: *populis*; referring to the "Astomi," a legendary race of people mentioned by Pliny the Elder who had no mouths and lived solely on the scent of roots, flowers, and wild apples.</note> peoples who live on scent alone, they will understand that what we are saying is true.

But let us descend to the nature of the spirit. The spirit is undoubtedly of a <term>Jovial</term> <note>relating to the planet Jupiter, considered the "Great Benefic" in astrology, associated with growth and health</note> quality, for it is infused into us during the time of Jupiter. It is also <term>solar</term> <note>relating to the Sun</note>. For Jupiter infuses the spirit insofar as he tempers within himself the immense power of the Sun. Furthermore, it is Jovial for another reason: because it is hot and moist, and it abounds in heat rather than moisture; it is born from the blood and is called a certain "bloody vapor." Again, because it glows and is exceedingly subtle and shining, and arises from the heart, it is undoubtedly judged to be solar.

<summary>Ficino advises on how to maintain the "spirit" through movement, fragrance, and variety to avoid Saturnian melancholy. He defines the biological spirit as a vapor derived from the blood, governed by the life-giving influences of Jupiter and the Sun.</summary>

<keywords>spiritus, astrology, Jupiter, Saturn, Venus, medical astrology, Pliny the Elder, Plato, humors, health and movement</keywords>

...and in a sense, a Venusian <term>virtue</term><note>Latin: *virtutem*. In this context, "virtue" refers to an inherent power, active quality, or medicinal efficacy rather than moral character.</note> within itself. For by a Venusian motion, it completely overflows, flows forth, transfers, and propagates offspring; it expands itself toward the pleasure of any sense and recoils from pain.

In summary, however, the spirit is considered Jovian <note>Associated with the planet Jupiter, symbolizing growth, health, and vital warmth.</note>, Venusian, and Solar <note>Associated with the Sun, the source of life and light.</note> insofar as it leads the body toward life, movement, and reproduction. Insofar as it serves the soul for sensation and imagination, it is judged to be Solar and Mercurial <note>Associated with the planet Mercury, representing speed, communication, and the movement of the mind.</note>—and indeed, it is Mercurial everywhere, since it is so mobile and so easily transformed and shaped. 

A healthy spirit does not commonly contain much of the nature of Saturn, Mars, or the Moon. Otherwise, from the former [Saturn] it would often become sluggish, from the second [Mars] furious, and from the latter [the Moon] it would be, in a sense, dull. 

¶ For this reason, lunar things—being somewhat thicker and more humid—are quite <note>The Latin *admodum* indicates "to a great degree," but the sentence breaks off at the end of the page.</note> [distant] from the subtle and volatile nature of the spirit...

<meta>This page continues a discussion on the relationship between planetary influences and the "spirit"—a subtle substance in the body that links the soul and the physical form. The previous page likely introduced the specific roles of planets like Mercury in governing mental states.</meta>

-># ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE<-

...to the animal spirit <note>original: "spiritui animali" — in Renaissance medicine, this was a "spirit" or subtle vapor that traveled through the nerves to facilitate thought and movement</note>; while Mercury is poorly positioned. He holds such great authority over the animal spirit that by his "caduceus" <note>the traditional staff carried by the god Mercury, often depicted with two snakes</note> he is said to be able at one moment to lull minds to sleep, and at another to rouse them. That is to say, depending on whether his planetary alignment is one way or another, he can wonderfully dull the intellect or sharpen it; weaken or strengthen it; and agitate or calm it. 

Therefore, whenever you desire to cultivate any particular spirit, you should not only observe that its patron planet is fortunate and powerful, but you should also choose a time when the Moon is favorably directed toward it. Now, the actual substance of the spirit is never properly created or restored by the influence of Saturn alone; rather, through Saturn, the spirit is always called back from external things to the innermost parts, and often from the lowest things to the highest. For this reason, Saturn leads us toward the contemplation of more secret and higher matters. 

Nevertheless, the power of Mars and Saturn can sometimes—though rarely—benefit the spirit like a medicine: either by warming it when necessary and by exciting and expanding it, or conversely, by restraining a spirit that is too volatile. Indeed, the nature of the spirit is created and restored primarily through those things which are in harmony with the other four planets <note>The Sun, Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury</note>. 

However, if you turn too strongly and without discernment toward "Solar" things <note>items or activities associated with the Sun</note>, you will eventually sharpen the spirit too much, drying it out and finally dissolving it. Similarly, if you turn toward "Venereal" things <note>associated with the planet Venus</note>, you will gradually liquefy or dull the spirit. If you rely only on "Mercurial" things, you will gain very little benefit. It will therefore be worth your while to use "Jovial" things <note>associated with the planet Jupiter</note> first and foremost, mixing the others into them in moderate amounts, and to more frequently use things that are common to all of these...

...things, or specifically those belonging to Jupiter. For these also are in a certain way common to all. Generally, however, those things pertain to all these <note>Referring to the celestial spirits or planetary influences</note> which possess a substance that is neither too fiery, nor entirely earthy, nor simply watery; neither of a very sharp nor very dull quality, but rather moderate: and smooth to the touch, and somewhat soft—or at least not hard or rough; likewise somewhat sweet to the taste, fragrant to the smell, pleasing to the sight, soothing and delightful to the ear, and joyful to the mind. 

A certain sweetness of flavor and grace is therefore common to all these things. If the sweetness is somewhat watery and simultaneously fatty, it pertains more to Venus. If it is somewhat tasteless, or slightly harsh <note>original: *austera*; referring to a dry or astringent quality often associated with unripe fruit</note>, it belongs rather to Mercury. To this belongs the substance of sweet almonds, pine nuts, hazelnuts, pistachios, starch, and licorice; raisins, egg yolks, the meat of pullets <note>young hens</note>, pheasants, peacocks, partridges, and similar things. Likewise the roots of ben <note>likely *Moringa* or *Behen*, medicinal roots used in syrups</note> and elecampane. Manna <note>A sweet resin gathered from trees, used as a mild laxative and sweetener</note> also belongs to Jupiter, provided it is strengthened by the infused power of the <term>myrobalan|a dried, plum-like fruit from India, highly prized in medieval pharmacy for balancing the humors</term>. -> ## ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS <-

...[joy] and sweetness proper to <term>Jupiter<note>In Renaissance astrology, Jupiter was known as the 'Great Benefic,' a planet associated with growth, healing, and a balanced, 'sweet' temperament.</note></term>, which certainly contributes above all else to the creation and restoration of the <term>spirit<note>original: 'spiritui'. Here, 'spirit' refers to a subtle vapor in the body that mediates between the physical body and the soul.</note></term>. Indeed, many things are "Jovial" (pertaining to Jupiter): everything we mentioned in the book *On Lengthening Life* as serving to preserve youth and being healthy for the elderly.

But if there is very little sweetness, and it contains much that is sharp and <term>astringent<note>original: 'stiptici'. In ancient medicine, this referred to substances that cause body tissues to contract or 'dry out.'</note></term>, or even a hint of bitterness, it is judged to be **Solar** (pertaining to the Sun). 

There is a similar logic and distinction regarding odors, for these are the siblings of tastes. The same applies to colors. Indeed, colors that are watery, white, green, somewhat saffron-colored, and similar to the colors of violets, roses, and lilies—as well as odors of that kind—relate to **Venus**, and the **Moon**, and **Mercury**. However, colors that are sapphire-like, which are also called "airy," and also those that are deep purple...

<summary>This page discusses how various tastes, smells, and colors correspond to the influence of specific planets, particularly how 'sweet' qualities are tied to Jupiter and sharp qualities to the Sun. It emphasizes how these sensory experiences can be used to restore the human 'spirit' and maintain health.</summary>
<keywords>astrological medicine, Jupiter, Solar, Venus, planetary correspondences, spiritus, color theory, odors, Marsilio Ficino</keywords>

...and clove <note>original: "chariophyllus"</note>, and musk; but these are linked to the Sun by their scent, not by their taste or appearance. Amber <note>likely referring to ambergris or precious resin</note> is mostly Solar <note>associated with the Sun</note> and Jovial <note>associated with Jupiter</note>. Saffron is Solar in all its aspects, although astrologers dedicate its color and scent to all the **Graces** <note>the three goddesses of charm, beauty, and creativity</note>. Taste, however, specifically belongs to the Sun. 

Finally, all fragrant and aromatic things, to the extent that they produce a pleasant scent, belong to Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury as much as to the Sun. However, among these, the sharper scents belong more to the Sun, while the more muted or soft ones belong rather to Venus and Mercury. To Jupiter belong those things that are properly balanced across all senses: smell, taste, hearing, sight, and touch. 

Pleasant and soothing sounds and songs also relate to all the **Graces** and to Mercury. But sounds that are truly threatening or mournful reveal the influence of Mars and Saturn. 

Do not be surprised that we attribute so much power to colors, smells, and voices. For tastes belong primarily to the <term>natural spirit</term> <note>the "spiritus," a subtle substance in the body believed to connect the soul and the physical organs</note>, while smells...

And as he himself turns out, in turn he creates, in a certain way, a corresponding disposition of the soul and an overall quality of the body. ¶ Finally, when he is first opportunely exposed to the <term>Graces</term> <note>original: "gratiis"; Ficino refers both to the mythological Three Graces and the "favors" or gifts radiated by the planets.</note> through those things which are specific to them—seeing as he was naturally in harmony with them—he immediately brings back wonderful gifts from those Graces through their rays, which are both vigorous everywhere and akin to him.

¶ Natural and even artificial things have hidden powers from the stars, through which they expose our <term>spirit</term> <note>In Renaissance medicine, the "spirit" was a subtle, airy substance that acted as a bridge between the physical body and the immaterial soul.</note> to those same stars. ¶ Chapter 12.

**C**ONCERNING the fact that we say the spirit is exposed to the Graces through things that belong to the Graces: we believe that it is adapted not only through qualities that are seen, heard, smelled, and tasted, but also through those that are touched. Remember, therefore, that:

| Quality | First Degree | Second Degree | Third Degree | Fourth Degree |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Hot** | Jupiter | Sun with Jupiter | Mars with the Sun | Mars |
| **Cold** | Venus | The Moon | The Moon with Saturn | Saturn |
| **Moist** | Mercury with Jupiter | Venus with the Moon | The Moon with Venus | The Moon (when joined to Venus and Mercury) |
| **Dry** | Jupiter | Mercury with the Sun | Sun with Mars | Mars with Saturn |

In summary, these are gathered from the qualities of the planets which <term>Ptolemy</term> <note>Claudius Ptolemy (c. 100–170 AD), the most influential astronomer and geographer of antiquity.</note> describes in his <term>Quadripartitum</term> <note>original: "qdriptito"; also known as the *Tetrabiblos*, the foundational text of Western astrology.</note>.
